# Balancing report

One section per scenario and mode, each with its own targets (plan section 7 and `docs/phase9-proposal.md`). `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --events` runs every scenario in the 60 week game; `--scenario <id>` runs one, `--mode long` or `--mode all` the long mode, and every other section is kept. The hand written tuning notes are at the end.

# Kantino

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario kantino`, engine 0.15.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (plan section 7)

Targets are judged on `validate_then_build`. Targets 2, 6, 7 and 8 are also shown for the hire variants, for information only: the hire is meant to pay rarely (decisions log), so those columns are not expected to hold. The margin is half the width of a 95% interval; differences between strategies are paired on the same seeds.

## Pricing (target 17)

`validate_then_build` at different prices, same 10000 seeds. Points against the default price, with the paired margin. The search reads only what the screen shows: trials in its market that said yes or "too expensive", and customers who left over the price. The recipe raises a fifth every 3 weeks until its first "too expensive", then goes one step back for good.

| Pricing | All | coworking | medical | small_offices |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| default price (validate_then_build) | 34.5% | 34.9% | 35.1% | 33.5% |

| 2 times the default | 30.6% (-3.9 ±1.2) | 73.0% (+38.0 ±1.9) | 8.5% (-26.5 ±1.8) | 9.5% (-24.0 ±1.8) |

## The freelancer (target 19)

Each freelancer run plays like `validate_then_build` until it books, on the same 10000 seeds. Points against plain play (paired margin, seeds). Split by the build hours of the must haves of the market the bot was working on when it booked; "never booked" seeds play exactly as plain. A developer is booked from the first week with a chosen market and unbuilt wanted features, on the wanted feature with the most hours left, until they have shipped; a salesperson from the first paying customer for a fixed number of weeks.

| Run | all | 85 h (small_offices) | 105 h (medical) | 135 h (coworking) | never booked | Booked | Median cost | Median weeks | Median hours of work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| freelance:compare validate_then_build_early_hire | -23.0 ±0.9 (10000) | - | - | - | -23.0 ±0.9 (10000) | 0.0% | - | - | - |

## Chasing invoices

The careful bot chases up to three overdue invoices a week, 1 h each (validate_then_build inbox:reader, 10000 runs). "Unchased" is what each chased invoice would have done without the chase (hidden truth): paid anyway, later; waited for a chase; or never paid. A chase changes nothing else in the game.

| Runs that chased | Chases per run | Chases per week played | Founder hours per run | Share of all planned hours | Unchased: paid anyway / waited for a chase / never |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 99.6% | 85.2 | 1.64 | 85.2 | 3.3% | 52.5% / 42.8% / 4.7% |

Against the same bot that never chases (same seeds): -27.1 points without chasing; won only with chasing 2912 seeds, only without 201. In the seeds won only without, the chasing run chased a median 120.0 times (120.0 h) against 99.0 in all runs.

## The hire

Each hire variant plays like `validate_then_build` until its hire rule fires, so on the same seed the runs are identical up to the search. "With a hire" and "without" split the variant's own runs; "same seeds, plain" is plain `validate_then_build` on the seeds where the variant hired, so the two win rates on the right compare the same games with and without the hire.

## Inbox policies

`validate_then_build` with four ways of answering the inbox, same 10000 seeds. Reading picks the answer each message deserves in the situation it arrives in (`readInbox`); blind effort takes the most effortful affordable choice without reading (`answerInbox`); always cheapest takes the choice with the fewest hours, then the least money (`cheapestInbox`); none leaves every message to its default.

| Policy | Win rate | Against none (points) | Won only with this policy | Won only with none |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| reading | 34.5% | 12.0 | 2149 | 951 |
| none | 22.5% | 0.0 | 0 | 0 |
| effort | 24.4% | 1.9 | 1436 | 1248 |
| cheapest | 20.9% | -1.6 | 1131 | 1292 |

## Inbox pool

## Strategies

- `random`: Spends a random 30 to 65 h on random actions in random markets, including tests, pre-sales and invoice chasing, and answers about half of the messages with a random choice. (2.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `build_only`: Builds every feature in list order and fixes bugs. Never talks to anyone. (0.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `sell_first`: Does outreach from week 1 without interviews, by phone in every market, then focuses on the market that asked for most demos. Builds the core feature, then the others in a random order. Chases three late invoices a week. (1.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build`: Interviews every market in week 1. In week 2 pitches one pre-sale batch in the market its interviews favour, runs landing page tests in the top two, and starts the feature everyone asks for. When those tests do not separate the two markets, it runs one larger test in each. Pitches every pre-sale batch its chosen market allows, one a week. Drops a tested market when another drew clearly more landing sign ups (1.5 standard errors), or after two pre-sale batches without a deposit. Builds what its market asks for, sells where its channels find the most leads (moving hours to a fresh channel when one tires), follows up with up to six open leads there a week, asks up to three lost leads or customers there a week why (a missing feature they name counts like a mention in an interview), and moves on after 12 weeks of selling without a customer. Sorts the books when the accountant asks, chases three late invoices a week, and reads the inbox (`readInbox`). (15.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and makes the one hire (a developer while wanted features are unbuilt, else a salesperson) once monthly revenue covers that salary, choosing the best impression. (12.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_early_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and searches for the one hire as soon as its own funnel (leads per hour on the best channel, share of closed leads won, current price) says a developer or salesperson would bring in more than their salary before the last week, assuming no churn and 8 weeks from extra outreach to payment, while cash and savings hold two months of that salary. Takes the role that pays back more, and hires the best impression if it still pays back at that skill. (9.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `presale_max`: Plays like validate_then_build, but pitches every pre-sale batch allowed: three in every market it has not dropped, from week 2, as hours allow. Ranks markets by deposits as well as interviews. (13.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `price_aware`: Plays like validate_then_build and searches for its price from what it sees in its own market. With two or more budget quotes from interviews there, it starts at 0.9 times their average and stays between 0.75 and 1.05 times it. After 2 trials there that said yes or no over the price, it raises the price by a fifth if nobody said no over the price, keeps it if a few did, and lowers it by a step if half did or a customer there left over the price. A price it lowered from is tried again only after two clean windows below it. (16.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `overworker`: Plays like validate_then_build, but plans 65 h every week. (2.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `playtest`: The hand playtest: 5 interviews per market in week 1, builds the three coworking features in weeks 2 to 4, 45 h of in person outreach to coworking in weeks 5 to 7, then every demo plus 15 h of outreach a week. (1.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `one_channel`: Plays like validate_then_build, but puts every outreach hour of a market on its one best channel each week, as every bot did before plan 7, even when that channel is worn out. (13.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `never_ask`: Plays like validate_then_build, but never asks a lost lead or customer why. It still reads the reasons some give unprompted. (15.4 s for 1000 runs)

# Kantino, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario kantino --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (long mode)

Targets 20 to 30 (`docs/phase9-proposal.md` 2.9 and Part C2), judged on `validate_then_build`, which builds a team in long mode. The margin is half the width of a 95% interval; differences between strategies are paired on the same seeds.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20 | Renting loses less than hiring for a burst of work, and hiring loses less than renting for steady work: for the build burst (from week 3 until it ships), a 20 h freelance developer beats a part time developer hire by at least 2 points; for steady selling (from the week the business can carry a part time salesperson by the careful rule, to the end), a part time salesperson hire beats a 20 h freelance salesperson by at least 2 points. Same seeds (on 10000 seeds) | burst: freelancer ahead by +0.0; steady: hire ahead by -2.4 | ±1.0, ±0.9 | no |
| 21 | build_only reaches Sustainable business in under 5% of runs; no strategy exceeds 60% | build_only 0.0%; top validate_then_build 44.7% | ±0.0; ±3.1 | yes |
| 22 | validate_then_build reaches it in 25 to 45% of runs | 44.7% | ±3.1 | yes |
| 23 | Median run length of losing runs (all strategies) is between week 30 and week 70 | week 35.0 | ±0.5 | yes |
| 24 | validate_then_build win rates across the three viable markets lie within 15 points (on 10000 seeds) | 5.5 points | ±2.4 | yes |

## Pricing (target 17)

`validate_then_build` at different prices, same 10000 seeds. Points against the default price, with the paired margin. The search reads only what the screen shows: trials in its market that said yes or "too expensive", and customers who left over the price. ## The freelancer (target 19)

Each freelancer run plays like `validate_then_build` until it books, on the same 10000 seeds. Points against plain play (paired margin, seeds). Split by the build hours of the must haves of the market the bot was working on when it booked; "never booked" seeds play exactly as plain. A developer is booked from the first week with a chosen market and unbuilt wanted features, on the wanted feature with the most hours left, until they have shipped; a salesperson from the first paying customer for a fixed number of weeks.

## Chasing invoices

The careful bot chases up to three overdue invoices a week, 1 h each (validate_then_build inbox:reader, 10000 runs). "Unchased" is what each chased invoice would have done without the chase (hidden truth): paid anyway, later; waited for a chase; or never paid. A chase changes nothing else in the game.

| Runs that chased | Chases per run | Chases per week played | Founder hours per run | Share of all planned hours | Unchased: paid anyway / waited for a chase / never |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 98.8% | 167.8 | 2.06 | 167.8 | 4.1% | 51.9% / 43.2% / 4.9% |

Against the same bot that never chases (same seeds): -33.7 points without chasing; won only with chasing 3477 seeds, only without 108. In the seeds won only without, the chasing run chased a median 215.5 times (215.5 h) against 198.0 in all runs.

## What each behaviour changed: validate_then_build

Same 10000 seeds, one behaviour switched off at a time. "Won only with" counts seeds the full strategy wins and this variant does not; "won only without" the other way round. The paired counts show how many seeds actually flipped; the margin of each change is in the targets table (target 13).

| Variant | Win rate | Change (points) | Won only with | Won only without | Bankrupt | Median losing run length |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| all behaviours | 43.0% | 0.0 | 0 | 0 | 38.8% | 52 |
| without inbox | 23.6% | -19.4 | 2514 | 578 | 57.9% | 48 |
| without chase | 9.3% | -33.7 | 3477 | 108 | 70.1% | 48 |
| without landing | 39.0% | -4.0 | 1765 | 1370 | 44.2% | 50 |
| without books | 39.3% | -3.6 | 1523 | 1161 | 45.2% | 44 |
| without followUps | 20.1% | -22.8 | 2713 | 429 | 58.9% | 51 |
| without all | 0.2% | -42.8 | 4279 | 1 | 99.2% | 37 |

## The hire

Each hire variant plays like `validate_then_build` until its hire rule fires, so on the same seed the runs are identical up to the search. "With a hire" and "without" split the variant's own runs; "same seeds, plain" is plain `validate_then_build` on the seeds where the variant hired, so the two win rates on the right compare the same games with and without the hire.

| Variant | Runs with a hire | Developer / salesperson | Median offer week | Win rate | Win rate with a hire | Win rate without | Same seeds, plain | Won only with the hire | Won only without | Median cost per customer the hire found | Median value of a customer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| validate_then_build_hire | 539 (53.9%) | 0 / 539 | 47 | 13.3% | 21.7% | 3.5% | 79.8% | 8 | 321 | 7,912 EUR | 2,431 EUR |
| validate_then_build_early_hire | 901 (90.1%) | 0 / 901 | 10 | 0.8% | 0.2% | 6.1% | 48.7% | 0 | 437 | 12,100 EUR | 540 EUR |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Runs | 829 | 66 | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| Win rate | 0.2% | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% |

## Inbox policies

`validate_then_build` with four ways of answering the inbox, same 10000 seeds. Reading picks the answer each message deserves in the situation it arrives in (`readInbox`); blind effort takes the most effortful affordable choice without reading (`answerInbox`); always cheapest takes the choice with the fewest hours, then the least money (`cheapestInbox`); none leaves every message to its default.

| Policy | Win rate | Against none (points) | Won only with this policy | Won only with none |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| reading | 43.0% | 19.4 | 2514 | 578 |
| none | 23.6% | 0.0 | 0 | 0 |
| effort | 30.7% | 7.1 | 1616 | 908 |
| cheapest | 22.4% | -1.2 | 973 | 1095 |

## Strategies

- `random`: Spends a random 30 to 65 h on random actions in random markets, including tests, pre-sales and invoice chasing, and answers about half of the messages with a random choice. (11.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `build_only`: Builds every feature in list order and fixes bugs. Never talks to anyone. (5.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `sell_first`: Does outreach from week 1 without interviews, by phone in every market, then focuses on the market that asked for most demos. Builds the core feature, then the others in a random order. Chases three late invoices a week. (7.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build`: Interviews every market in week 1. In week 2 pitches one pre-sale batch in the market its interviews favour, runs landing page tests in the top two, and starts the feature everyone asks for. When those tests do not separate the two markets, it runs one larger test in each. Pitches every pre-sale batch its chosen market allows, one a week. Drops a tested market when another drew clearly more landing sign ups (1.5 standard errors), or after two pre-sale batches without a deposit. Builds what its market asks for, sells on its best channel, follows up with up to six open leads there a week, and moves on after 12 weeks of selling without a customer. Sorts the books when the accountant asks, chases three late invoices a week, and reads the inbox (`readInbox`). In long mode it also builds a team. It looks for a hire once its cash and savings grew over the last four weeks, would pay what the hire adds to the monthly loss for 6 months, and at least 26 weeks would be left once the hire starts: a developer while the features its market asks for need 40 h or more, else a salesperson, full time, the best impression. The next search waits until the newest hire has worked 8 weeks. The newest hire is let go once the runway shown falls below 3 months, and a developer once every feature has shipped. It takes a bigger office when its offer arrives and the reason is real: its hiring rule wants someone and every desk or place is taken, or, for the first office, three months that paid all costs; and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of today's costs. (62.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and makes the one hire (a developer while wanted features are unbuilt, else a salesperson) once monthly revenue covers that salary, choosing the best impression. (54.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_early_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and searches for the one hire as soon as its own funnel (leads per hour on the best channel, share of closed leads won, current price) says a developer or salesperson would bring in more than their salary before the last week, assuming no churn and 8 weeks from extra outreach to payment, while cash and savings hold two months of that salary. Takes the role that pays back more, and hires the best impression if it still pays back at that skill. (12.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `presale_max`: Plays like validate_then_build, but pitches every pre-sale batch allowed: three in every market it has not dropped, from week 2, as hours allow. Ranks markets by deposits as well as interviews. (45.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `price_aware`: Plays like validate_then_build and searches for its price from what it sees in its own market. With two or more budget quotes from interviews there, it starts at 0.9 times their average and stays between 0.75 and 1.05 times it. After 2 trials there that said yes or no over the price, it raises the price by a fifth if nobody said no over the price, keeps it if a few did, and lowers it by a step if half did or a customer there left over the price. A price it lowered from is tried again only after two clean windows below it. (54.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `overworker`: Plays like validate_then_build, but plans 65 h every week. (4.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `playtest`: The hand playtest: 5 interviews per market in week 1, builds the three coworking features in weeks 2 to 4, 45 h of in person outreach to coworking in weeks 5 to 7, then every demo plus 15 h of outreach a week. (3.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_solo`: Plays like validate_then_build and never hires or rents anyone: the bootstrapped solo founder. (53.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_savings_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and makes its first hire on savings: it looks for one as soon as building starts (week 3), while cash and savings hold 3 months of the salary, and keeps that hire until the runway shown falls below 1 month. Later hires follow the reference rule. (22.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_part_time`: Plays like validate_then_build, with the same team rule, but hires only part time (20 h at half the salary). (56.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_freelance_first`: Plays like validate_then_build, and until its first hire is on the way rents a 20 h freelancer in the role that limits it. A developer for the build, as a burst: from week 3, while the features its market asks for need 40 h or more and cash and savings cover the whole build left at the freelancer's rate plus 3 months of the current loss. A salesperson for the pipeline, steady: from the first paying customer, while cash and savings would pay the added loss for 3 months. Gives notice when the role needed changes, the runway shown falls below 3 months, or the reference rule starts a search for a hire. (47.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_expand`: Plays like validate_then_build, and from week 53, once its leads per outreach hour in its market over the last 8 weeks of selling fall below 60% of its best 8 weeks so far, opens the best ranked other market: interviews there until it has heard 12, builds what they ask for (up to 20 h a week itself), then sells there with half its outreach, and gives it up after 16 weeks of selling there without a customer. Its team rule serves the second market: a developer while its features need 40 h, building them first, then a salesperson selling there. (54.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_never_move`: Plays like validate_then_build but declines every office offer (plan 3, target O1): it stays where it started, desks or not. (53.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_always_move`: Plays like validate_then_build but accepts every office offer at once (plan 3, target O2), whether or not it needs the room or has the money. (22.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_expand_solo`: Plays like validate_then_build_expand, but never hires or rents anyone: the founder opens the second market alone. (50.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_founder_chases`: Plays like validate_then_build, but the founder keeps chasing late invoices when someone is on the team, instead of handing it to them. (54.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `raise_on_traction`: Plays like validate_then_build, and raises once it has traction: an angel round once monthly revenue covers the company's costs and grew in each of the last two months, with at least 20 weeks of runway; a seed round once monthly revenue reaches the seed bar and grew in each of the last two months. While a round is open it pitches 10 h a week, books a second look with every interested investor and accepts the first term sheet; a round that runs out of investors is stopped and tried again after 12 weeks. With investors' money it hires while the money would last 9 months after the hire. Sends a proper update every month. (60.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `raise_early`: Plays like raise_on_traction, but starts an angel round in week 3, on interviews and sign ups, starts again at once when a round runs out of investors, and starts a seed round as soon as the angel money is in. (53.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `raise_always`: Plays like raise_on_traction, but starts an angel round at its first paying customer, whether or not it needs the money. (51.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `raise_late`: Plays like validate_then_build, and starts an angel round only once the runway shown falls below 12 weeks; it keeps that round open to the end, as a founder who needs the money does. (43.4 s for 1000 runs)

## The freelancer (target 19)

Each freelancer run plays like `validate_then_build` until it books, on the same 10000 seeds. Points against plain play (paired margin, seeds). Split by the build hours of the must haves of the market the bot was working on when it booked; "never booked" seeds play exactly as plain. A developer is booked from the first week with a chosen market and unbuilt wanted features, on the wanted feature with the most hours left, until they have shipped; a salesperson from the first paying customer for a fixed number of weeks.

| Run | all | 85 h (small_crews) | 105 h (maintenance) | 135 h (contractors) | never booked | Booked | Median cost | Median weeks | Median hours of work |

## Chasing invoices

The careful bot chases up to three overdue invoices a week, 1 h each (validate_then_build inbox:reader, 10000 runs). "Unchased" is what each chased invoice would have done without the chase (hidden truth): paid anyway, later; waited for a chase; or never paid. A chase changes nothing else in the game.

| Runs that chased | Chases per run | Chases per week played | Founder hours per run | Share of all planned hours | Unchased: paid anyway / waited for a chase / never |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 98.8% | 79.1 | 1.62 | 79.1 | 3.2% | 57.5% / 38.8% / 3.8% |

Against the same bot that never chases (same seeds): -19.9 points without chasing; won only with chasing 2151 seeds, only without 162. In the seeds won only without, the chasing run chased a median 123.0 times (123.0 h) against 93.0 in all runs.

## Inbox policies

`validate_then_build` with four ways of answering the inbox, same 10000 seeds. Reading picks the answer each message deserves in the situation it arrives in (`readInbox`); blind effort takes the most effortful affordable choice without reading (`answerInbox`); always cheapest takes the choice with the fewest hours, then the least money (`cheapestInbox`); none leaves every message to its default.

| Policy | Win rate | Against none (points) | Won only with this policy | Won only with none |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| reading | 26.1% | 11.6 | 1849 | 688 |
| none | 14.5% | 0.0 | 0 | 0 |
| effort | 17.8% | 3.3 | 1203 | 873 |
| cheapest | 13.7% | -0.7 | 875 | 949 |

## Strategies

- `random`: Spends a random 30 to 65 h on random actions in random markets, including tests, pre-sales and invoice chasing, and answers about half of the messages with a random choice. (10.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `build_only`: Builds every feature in list order and fixes bugs. Never talks to anyone. (4.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `sell_first`: Does outreach from week 1 without interviews, by phone in every market, then focuses on the market that asked for most demos. Builds the core feature, then the others in a random order. Chases three late invoices a week. (6.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build`: Interviews every market in week 1. In week 2 pitches one pre-sale batch in the market its interviews favour, runs landing page tests in the top two, and starts the feature everyone asks for. When those tests do not separate the two markets, it runs one larger test in each. Pitches every pre-sale batch its chosen market allows, one a week. Drops a tested market when another drew clearly more landing sign ups (1.5 standard errors), or after two pre-sale batches without a deposit. Builds what its market asks for, sells on its best channel, follows up with up to six open leads there a week, and moves on after 12 weeks of selling without a customer. Sorts the books when the accountant asks, chases three late invoices a week, and reads the inbox (`readInbox`). (28.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and makes the one hire (a developer while wanted features are unbuilt, else a salesperson) once monthly revenue covers that salary, choosing the best impression. (23.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `validate_then_build_early_hire`: Plays like validate_then_build, and searches for the one hire as soon as its own funnel (leads per hour on the best channel, share of closed leads won, current price) says a developer or salesperson would bring in more than their salary before the last week, assuming no churn and 8 weeks from extra outreach to payment, while cash and savings hold two months of that salary. Takes the role that pays back more, and hires the best impression if it still pays back at that skill. (14.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `presale_max`: Plays like validate_then_build, but pitches every pre-sale batch allowed: three in every market it has not dropped, from week 2, as hours allow. Ranks markets by deposits as well as interviews. (22.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `price_aware`: Plays like validate_then_build and searches for its price from what it sees in its own market. With two or more budget quotes from interviews there, it starts at 0.9 times their average and stays between 0.75 and 1.05 times it. After 2 trials there that said yes or no over the price, it raises the price by a fifth if nobody said no over the price, keeps it if a few did, and lowers it by a step if half did or a customer there left over the price. A price it lowered from is tried again only after two clean windows below it. (23.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `overworker`: Plays like validate_then_build, but plans 65 h every week. (4.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `playtest`: The hand playtest: 5 interviews per market in week 1, builds the three contractors features in weeks 2 to 4, 45 h of in person outreach to contractors in weeks 5 to 7, then every demo plus 15 h of outreach a week. (2.5 s for 1000 runs)

# Gratwanderung, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario gratwanderung --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (70.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (43.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (5.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (45.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (2.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (45.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (36.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (45.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (5.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (34.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (39.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (39.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (44.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (42.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (44.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (41.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (42.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (17.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (30.5 s for 1000 runs)

# Hinterhof, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario hinterhof --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 36.4% | ±3.0 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_always_strict 39.3%; no tests 19.7% | ±3.0; ±2.5 | no |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 13.2 points (evening_locals 30.5%, office_lunch 43.7%, tourists 34.7%) |  | yes |
| T4 | The first guest from the viable segment is served by week 12 in the median winning run | week 12.0 |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 90.4%; year 2 100.0% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +3.0, blind effort +0.4 | ±1.4 | yes |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | -1.6 | ±1.4 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -17.3 | ±2.4 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -25.5, under -36.4 | ±2.7, ±3.0 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -11.7 | ±2.1 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by +2.9 overall; evening_locals +10.4, office_lunch +2.1, tourists -3.2 | ±1.8 | yes |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 142.9 s per 1000 runs |  | yes |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 35.7%; gap -0.7 | ±3.0; ±1.5 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent +4.0 (signed in 27.3%); seasonal +1.8 (signed in 27.9%); part time +1.9 (signed in 37.8%) | ±2.3; ±1.6; ±1.7 | yes |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan (a second loan for the own fit-out) is a real choice: borrowing and not borrowing are within 5 points of each other | second loan +2.3 against none (second loan taken in 60.0% of runs) | ±1.3 | yes |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -27.9 | ±2.8 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 93.6%, careful 60.7% (gap +32.9); careful loan-caused misses 1.1% | ±3.4; ±0.6 | yes |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 100.0% borrowed; median interest and fees 2223 EUR |  | yes |
| T19 | O1. Moving at the right time costs little: the careful bot with its stage rules loses at most 1 point against never moving | +0.0 | ±0.7 | yes |
| T20 | O2. Moving early loses: accepting every offer at once loses to the careful bot by at least 3 points | -36.4 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T21 | O3. No stage is always right: among the careful bot's wins, each of stages 3 to 5 offered in at least 5% of them is taken in some and declined in others (taken in at most 90% of the wins where it was offered); stages offered less often are not judged (restated in tuning 1) | stage 3 taken in 45.1% of 364; stage 4 taken in 7.9% of 126; stage 5 never offered |  | yes |
| T22 | E1. The established look comes once the company has made it: the careful bot reaches it in at least 40% of its winning runs, in clearly fewer of its losing runs, and in year 2 (median week after 52) | 95.6% of 364 wins, 3.3% of 636 losses; median week 50 | ±2.1, ±1.4; ±1.5 | no |

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (142.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (89.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (60.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (8.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (109.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (111.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (80.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (108.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (4.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (98.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (103.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (106.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (105.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (105.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (108.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (107.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (103.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (14.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (106.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (41.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (30.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (77.5 s for 1000 runs)

# Seeblick, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario seeblick --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 36.2% | ±3.0 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_naive_careful 38.4%; no tests 0.6% | ±3.0; ±0.5 | yes |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 12.7 points (groups 29.4%, leisure 42.2%, project_guests 37.1%) |  | yes |
| T4 | The first guest from the viable segment is served by week 12 in the median winning run | week 6.0 |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 96.5%; year 2 99.5% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +6.8, blind effort +6.1 | ±2.2 | no |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | -1.3 | ±1.8 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -31.3 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -3.2, under -23.2 | ±2.2, ±2.7 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -3.4 | ±2.0 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by +1.2 overall; groups +7.3, leisure +5.0, project_guests -9.4 | ±2.4 | yes |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 83.1 s per 1000 runs |  | yes |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 38.4%; gap +2.2 | ±3.0; ±2.5 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent -; seasonal -; part time +1.3 (signed in 7.6%) | ; ; ±2.6 | no |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan (a second loan for the own fit-out) is a real choice: borrowing and not borrowing are within 5 points of each other | second loan +0.2 against none (second loan taken in 25.6% of runs) | ±1.0 | yes |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -27.9 | ±2.8 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 93.1%, careful 57.1% (gap +36.0); careful loan-caused misses 1.4% | ±3.4; ±0.7 | yes |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 100.0% borrowed; median interest and fees 1462 EUR |  | yes |
| T19 | O1. Moving at the right time costs little: the careful bot with its stage rules loses at most 1 point against never moving | +0.1 | ±0.2 | yes |
| T20 | O2. Moving early loses: accepting every offer at once loses to the careful bot by at least 3 points | -36.2 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T21 | O3. No stage is always right: among the careful bot's wins, each of stages 3 to 5 offered in at least 5% of them is taken in some and declined in others (taken in at most 90% of the wins where it was offered); stages offered less often are not judged (restated in tuning 1) | stage 3 taken in 8.6% of 362; stage 4 never offered; stage 5 never offered |  | yes |
| T22 | E1. The established look comes once the company has made it: the careful bot reaches it in at least 40% of its winning runs, in clearly fewer of its losing runs, and in year 2 (median week after 52) | 58.6% of 362 wins, 0.6% of 638 losses; median week 57 | ±5.1, ±0.6; ±1.5 | yes |

Interviews and the player's own spending do not count. The stricter column counts only news that asks for a decision: a new message, a lead that moved stage, an experiment result, a lost customer or invoice, or candidates.

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (83.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (33.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (65.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (35.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (9.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (56.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (52.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (53.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (5.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (44.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (51.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (53.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (52.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (50.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (51.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (50.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (52.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (12.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (48.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (22.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (19.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (36.0 s for 1000 runs)

# Rückenwind, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario rueckenwind --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 45.2% | ±3.1 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_price_blind 48.2%; no tests 2.2% | ±3.1; ±0.9 | yes |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 8.9 points (hotel_guests 40.1%, learners 46.5%, wind_regulars 49.0%) |  | yes |
| T4 | The first guest from the viable segment is served by week 12 in the median winning run | week 8.0 |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 95.0%; year 2 91.3% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +2.7, blind effort -4.4 | ±1.5 | yes |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | -3.0 | ±1.8 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -25.8 | ±2.9 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -41.9, under -25.6 | ±3.1, ±2.7 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -37.6 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by +0.1 overall; hotel_guests +3.2, learners +5.7, wind_regulars -8.2 | ±2.0 | yes |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 203.9 s per 1000 runs |  | no |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 44.5%; gap -0.7 | ±3.1; ±1.3 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent +5.3 (signed in 69.9%); seasonal +5.1 (signed in 89.9%); part time +6.3 (signed in 69.5%) | ±2.4; ±1.9; ±2.3 | yes |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan (a second loan for the own fit-out) is a real choice: borrowing and not borrowing are within 5 points of each other | second loan +0.0 against none (second loan taken in 41.3% of runs) | ±0.0 | yes |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -12.8 | ±2.7 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 53.7%, careful 31.4% (gap +22.3); careful loan-caused misses 0.3% | ±4.2; ±0.3 | yes |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 69.1% borrowed; median interest and fees 1601 EUR |  | yes |
| T19 | O1. Moving at the right time costs little: the careful bot with its stage rules loses at most 1 point against never moving | +2.8 | ±1.3 | yes |
| T20 | O2. Moving early loses: accepting every offer at once loses to the careful bot by at least 3 points | -39.0 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T21 | O3. No stage is always right: among the careful bot's wins, each of stages 3 to 5 offered in at least 5% of them is taken in some and declined in others (taken in at most 90% of the wins where it was offered); stages offered less often are not judged (restated in tuning 1) | stage 3 taken in 20.1% of 452; stage 4 taken in 30.9% of 68; stage 5 taken in 2.1% of 47 |  | yes |
| T22 | E1. The established look comes once the company has made it: the careful bot reaches it in at least 40% of its winning runs, in clearly fewer of its losing runs, and in year 2 (median week after 52) | 47.6% of 452 wins, 4.7% of 548 losses; median week 91 | ±4.6, ±1.8; ±0.5 | yes |
| W1 | Selling for every kind of day pays: the careful bot beats a bot that offers only wind products, and a bot that offers only calm products, by at least 3 points each | wind only -22.9, calm only -39.5 | ±2.8, ±3.0 | yes |

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (189.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (136.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (66.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (110.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (47.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (121.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (76.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (203.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (9.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (196.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (158.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (140.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (131.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (129.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (128.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (130.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (120.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (65.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (132.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (63.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (125.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (136.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (120.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_wind_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but builds only products for wind (good or strong). (72.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_calm_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but builds only calm-water products (calm or light wind). (67.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_courses`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells hire and tours, never a course. (89.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_trips_always`: Plays like tourism_careful but runs a kite trip for a single student. (126.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_trips_never`: Plays like tourism_careful but never runs a kite trip. (121.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_tender_higher`: Plays like tourism_careful but always bids higher in the beach tender. (124.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_tender_same`: Plays like tourism_careful but always bids as before in the beach tender. (123.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_efoil_spring`: Plays like tourism_careful but builds the eFoil fleet in the first spring, with the bank. (115.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (93.2 s for 1000 runs)

# Tiefgang, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario tiefgang --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 35.9% | ±3.0 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_naive_careful 38.4%; no tests 5.1% | ±3.0; ±1.4 | yes |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 26.2 points (certified_divers 47.5%, dive_clubs 21.3%, resort_beginners 39.8%) |  | no |
| T4 | The first guest from the viable segment is served by week 12 in the median winning run | week 12.0 |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 88.5%; year 2 94.7% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +3.4, blind effort -5.1 | ±1.9 | yes |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | +0.0 | ±1.6 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -14.8 | ±2.6 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -30.5, under -19.0 | ±2.9, ±2.7 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -27.4 | ±2.8 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by -0.8 overall; certified_divers -5.0, dive_clubs -8.2, resort_beginners +10.6 | ±2.2 | yes |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 148.6 s per 1000 runs |  | yes |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 38.4%; gap +2.5 | ±3.0; ±2.0 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent +14.6 (signed in 62.9%); seasonal +13.0 (signed in 100.0%); part time +9.1 (signed in 72.7%) | ±3.3; ±2.4; ±2.5 | yes |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan (a second loan for the own fit-out) is a real choice: borrowing and not borrowing are within 5 points of each other | second loan +0.0 against none (second loan taken in 4.1% of runs) | ±0.0 | yes |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -3.5 | ±2.6 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 39.2%, careful 6.0% (gap +33.2); careful loan-caused misses 0.0% | ±3.4; ±0.0 | yes |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 27.7% borrowed; median interest and fees 280 EUR |  | yes |
| T19 | O1. Moving at the right time costs little: the careful bot with its stage rules loses at most 1 point against never moving | -0.1 | ±0.2 | yes |
| T20 | O2. Moving early loses: accepting every offer at once loses to the careful bot by at least 3 points | -11.7 | ±2.3 | yes |
| T21 | O3. No stage is always right: among the careful bot's wins, each of stages 3 to 5 offered in at least 5% of them is taken in some and declined in others (taken in at most 90% of the wins where it was offered); stages offered less often are not judged (restated in tuning 1) | stage 3 taken in 10.6% of 359; stage 4 taken in 93.8% of 16 (not judged); stage 5 never offered |  | yes |
| T22 | E1. The established look comes once the company has made it: the careful bot reaches it in at least 40% of its winning runs, in clearly fewer of its losing runs, and in year 2 (median week after 52) | 72.4% of 359 wins, 5.6% of 641 losses; median week 88 | ±4.6, ±1.8; ±1.5 | yes |
| D1 | The famous site is not the business: a bot that sells only to certified divers (wreck and shore dives, no try dives or entry courses) loses at least 3 points to the careful bot | -13.7 | ±3.2 | yes |

| D3 | Owning is risky: always_buy ends bankrupt or with the loan called at least 10 points more often than always_share, and its median final money net of the boat (cash plus resale value less the loan left) among its wins is the highest of the three | failed 55.0% against 9.3% (gap +45.7); median net in wins always_buy 129,657, always_lease 95,338, always_share 102,103 EUR | ±3.6 | yes |
| D4 | The ladder pays: a bot that sells try dives but no courses loses at least 3 points | -2.9 | ±2.9 | yes |

## Pacing in weeks 1 to 20

Share of weeks with at least one noteworthy thing: a new message, new leads, a lead that moved, an experiment result, a shipped feature, a payment, a churned customer, or candidates. Interviews and the player's own spending do not count. The stricter column counts only news that asks for a decision: a new message, a lead that moved stage, an experiment result, a lost customer or invoice, or candidates.

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (148.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (132.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (64.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (107.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (71.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (101.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (94.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (101.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (5.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (97.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (98.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (106.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (102.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (100.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (102.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (98.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (102.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (91.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (103.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (88.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (97.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (103.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (91.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_certified_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only to certified divers: wreck and shore dives, no try dives or entry courses. (63.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_ladder`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells try dives and no course whose licence another product needs. (90.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_buy`: Plays like tourism_careful but buys the boat at once, with the bank, and never leases or shares. (81.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_lease`: Plays like tourism_careful but always leases the boat. (95.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_share`: Plays like tourism_careful but always buys seats on the partner boat. (98.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (81.1 s for 1000 runs)

# Leinen los, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario leinenlos --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 47.7% | ±3.1 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_gulet_lease 50.1%; no tests 15.8% | ±3.1; ±2.3 | no |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 65.7 points (cabin_guests 79.9%, private_charters 49.4%, retreats 14.3%) |  | no |
| T4 | The first guest from the viable segment is served in the first season's first month (by week 35) in the median winning run (restated in tuning 1) | week 32.0 |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 85.2%; year 2 82.7% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +12.8, blind effort -5.4 | ±2.4 | yes |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | -2.1 | ±1.6 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -25.4 | ±2.8 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -11.6, under +0.0 | ±2.2, ±0.0 | no |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -4.5 | ±1.7 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by +2.3 overall; cabin_guests +5.0, private_charters -2.8, retreats +4.4 | ±2.2 | no |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 99.9 s per 1000 runs |  | yes |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 48.2%; gap +0.5 | ±3.1; ±1.5 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent -; seasonal -; part time - | ; ;  | no |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan (a second loan for the own fit-out) is a real choice: borrowing and not borrowing are within 5 points of each other | second loan +0.0 against none (second loan taken in 0.0% of runs) | ±0.0 | no |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -0.8 | ±2.1 | no |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 0.0%, careful 0.0% (gap +0.0); careful loan-caused misses 0.0% | ±0.0; ±0.0 | no |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | - |  | not measured |
| T19 | O1. Moving at the right time costs little: the careful bot with its stage rules loses at most 1 point against never moving | -1.1 | ±0.9 | yes |
| T20 | O2. Moving early loses: accepting every offer at once loses to the careful bot by at least 3 points | -5.3 | ±2.0 | yes |
| T21 | O3. No stage is always right: among the careful bot's wins, each of stages 3 to 5 offered in at least 5% of them is taken in some and declined in others (taken in at most 90% of the wins where it was offered); stages offered less often are not judged (restated in tuning 1) | stage 3 taken in 93.3% of 327; stage 4 taken in 74.8% of 226; stage 5 never offered |  | no |
| T22 | E1. The established look comes once the company has made it: the careful bot reaches it in at least 40% of its winning runs, in clearly fewer of its losing runs, and in year 2 (median week after 52) | 52.2% of 477 wins, 1.0% of 523 losses; median week 55 | ±4.5, ±0.8; ±1.5 | yes |
| G1 | No boat option is always right: on paired seeds, each of gulet_buy, gulet_lease, always_manage has the best result of the three in at least 15% of the seeds where they differ (money counted net of the boat: cash plus its resale value less the loan left) | gulet_buy 14.9%, gulet_lease 37.2%, always_manage 48.0% of 740 seeds |  | no |
| G2 | Owning is risky: gulet_buy ends bankrupt or with the loan called at least 10 points more often than always_manage, and its median final money net of the boat (cash plus resale value less the loan left) among its wins is the highest of the three | failed 48.3% against 15.4% (gap +32.9); median net in wins gulet_buy 136,255, gulet_lease 118,899, always_manage 87,346 EUR | ±3.8 | yes |
| G3 | Holding weeks is a decision: a bot that holds every peak week for charters and a bot that never holds each lose to the careful bot by at least 2 points | hold every peak week -24.3, never hold -0.5 | ±2.9, ±0.8 | no |
| G4 | Sailing half-empty beats cancelling: always sailing beats a minimum of six cabins by at least 3 points (restated in tuning 1: a trap whose lesson the post-mortem shows, each cancelled week with what it gave up and what it saved) | always sailing ahead by +6.2 overall; cabin_guests +3.2, private_charters +11.6, retreats +4.1 | ±1.8 | yes |
