## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (99.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (81.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (73.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (71.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (26.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (67.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (65.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (66.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (5.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (58.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (44.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (72.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (68.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (68.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (69.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (66.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (68.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (67.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (69.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (67.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (68.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (72.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (71.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_manage`: Plays like tourism_careful but always manages the owner's gulet. (75.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_gulet_lease`: Plays like tourism_careful but always leases the gulet. (59.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_gulet_buy`: Plays like tourism_careful but buys a gulet as soon as it can (the company first), with the bank. (62.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_hold_all_peak`: Plays like tourism_careful but holds every peak week for charters. (67.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_hold_never`: Plays like tourism_careful but never holds a week. (68.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_sail_always`: Plays like tourism_careful but sails with any number of cabins. (68.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_sail_six`: Plays like tourism_careful but sails only with six cabins or more. (61.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (54.3 s for 1000 runs)

# Ausgesperrt, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario ausgesperrt --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 43.0% | ±3.1 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_price_blind 50.9%; no tests 31.0% | ±3.1; ±2.9 | no |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 32.1 points (companies 24.2%, locals 56.3%, tourists 49.1%) | ±7.0 | no |
| T4 | The first viable-segment group is served by the twelfth week from opening in the median winning run | week 2.0 from opening |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 100.0%; year 2 100.0% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +12.7, blind effort +8.0 | reading ±2.5; blind limit ±1.9 | no |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | -7.9 | ±2.5 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -17.6 | ±2.7 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -43.0, under -42.2 | ±3.1, ±3.1 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -32.4 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by -1.8 overall; companies +0.9, locals +3.2, tourists -10.1 | ±2.3 | no |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 325.1 s per 1000 runs |  | no |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 37.9%; gap -5.1 | ±3.0; ±2.2 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent +3.7 (signed in 88.1%); seasonal +0.3 (signed in 88.2%); part time +7.1 (signed in 91.3%) | ±2.5; ±2.1; ±2.5 | no |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan for later rooms is a real choice: borrowing and funding later rooms from retained cash are within 5 points of each other | second loan +6.7 against none (second loan taken in 99.9% of runs) | ±2.4 | yes |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -9.2 | ±2.6 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 38.1%, careful 39.2% (gap -1.1); careful loan-caused misses 1.8% | ±4.3; ±0.8 | no |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 100.0% borrowed; median interest and fees 3983 EUR |  | yes |

| X1 | Products have a life: never building a second room loses at least 5 points, and building a new room every three months loses at least 3 | one room -42.2; quarterly +4.6 | ±3.1; ±2.1 | no |

## Pacing in weeks 1 to 20

Interviews and the player's own spending do not count. The stricter column counts only news that asks for a decision: a new message, a lead that moved stage, an experiment result, a lost customer or invoice, or candidates.

## Strategies

- `leisure_one_room`: Never builds a second room; still maintains the first. (225.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `leisure_quarterly_rooms`: Starts another room every thirteen weeks from opening when it can finance it. (325.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `leisure_theme_blind`: Chooses room themes from a seeded order without matching the market. (289.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `leisure_no_daytime`: Closes weekday daytime sales and keeps the other careful policies. (210.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `leisure_vr_first_year`: Adds the virtual room in the first year with bank help when needed. (219.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `leisure_no_later_loan`: Keeps the careful startup loan policy but funds every later room from retained cash. (230.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (280.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (202.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (106.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (122.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (237.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (277.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (194.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (274.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (9.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (238.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (257.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (255.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (270.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (283.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (274.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (279.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (283.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (58.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (277.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (58.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (245.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (249.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (233.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (206.0 s for 1000 runs)

# Fabriketage, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario fabriketage --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 39.5% | ±3.0 | yes |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_permanent 40.7%; no tests 0.0% | ±3.0; ±0.0 | yes |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 38.9 points (companies 54.3%, private_parties 47.2%, weddings 15.4%) | ±6.6 | no |
| T4 | The first viable-segment group is served by the twelfth week from opening in the median winning run | week 2.0 from opening |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 87.5%; year 2 97.8% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +10.9, blind effort +8.8 | reading ±2.0; blind limit ±1.7 | no |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | +1.2 | ±1.6 | yes |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -30.0 | ±2.9 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -38.3, under -3.8 | ±3.0, ±1.6 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -26.7 | ±2.8 | yes |
| T11 | The cancellation policy is a real decision: always strict and always flexible each win in some segment split, and neither is ahead by more than 5 points overall | strict ahead by -1.0 overall; companies -1.5, private_parties +1.7, weddings -3.5 | ±1.7 | no |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 134.1 s per 1000 runs |  | yes |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 37.0%; gap -2.5 | ±3.0; ±1.9 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent +0.3 (signed in 29.2%); seasonal -1.8 (signed in 33.5%); part time -5.0 (signed in 43.8%) | ±1.2; ±1.8; ±2.5 | no |
| T15 | L1. Optional borrowing and retained-cash funding of later venue investments are within 5 points of each other | second loan +0.0 against none (second loan taken in 0.0% of runs) | ±0.0 | no |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -39.5 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 100.0%, careful 57.2% (gap +42.8); careful loan-caused misses 0.8% | ±3.1; ±0.6 | yes |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 100.0% borrowed; median interest and fees 5412 EUR |  | yes |

| V1 | Holds are a decision: granting every hold freely and granting none each lose at least 2 points to careful | free -6.6; none -5.5 | ±1.9; ±1.9 | yes |

## Strategies

- `venue_no_later_loan`: Keeps the venue startup loan but funds later investments from retained cash. (134.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `venue_free_holds`: Grants every date hold freely. (122.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `venue_no_holds`: Grants no date holds. (96.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `venue_companies_only`: Sells only company event packages. (71.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `venue_weddings_only`: Sells only wedding packages. (37.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `venue_no_insulation`: Offers late events but never installs sound insulation. (104.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `venue_roof_first_year`: Builds the roof terrace in the first year. (75.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (106.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (55.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (17.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (100.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (13.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (96.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (61.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (101.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (6.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (80.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (94.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (96.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (98.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (100.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (99.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (102.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (100.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (14.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (99.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (14.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (94.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (8.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (8.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (64.3 s for 1000 runs)

# Schweißperle, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario schweissperle --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Targets (tourism)

The tourism target template (`docs/tourism-proposal.md` section 13), judged on `tourism_careful`; differences are paired on the same seeds. Target 6 (the inbox) needs the inbox part and is reported with the scenario.

| # | Target | Value | Margin | Met |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | tourism_careful wins (Sustainable or Growing) 25 to 45% of runs | 55.1% | ±3.1 | no |
| T2 | No strategy wins over 60%; tourism_no_tests under 5% | top tourism_naive_careful 60.8%; no tests 1.5% | ±3.0; ±0.8 | yes |
| T3 | tourism_careful win rates across the viable segments lie within 15 points | 13.9 points (corporate 63.3%, yoga_crowd 49.4%, young_professionals 52.2%) | ±7.5 | yes |
| T4 | The first viable-segment group is served by the twelfth week from opening in the median winning run | week 2.0 from opening |  | yes |
| T5 | Decision news in at least 85% of weeks 1 to 20 and 70% of weeks 53 to 104 | 86.3%; year 2 92.1% |  | yes |
| T6 | Reading the inbox pays: tourism_careful beats leaving every message to its default by at least 3 points, and blind effort gains at most a third of that | reading +26.0, blind effort +17.7 | reading ±2.9; blind limit ±2.1 | no |
| T7 | Seasonal pricing pays: tourism_careful (prices from hints per season) beats tourism_price_blind by 2 to 10 points | -3.9 | ±1.7 | no |
| T8 | Platform dependence is a trap: tourism_platform_only loses at least 3 points to the careful mix | -28.0 | ±3.0 | yes |
| T9 | Staffing is a decision: over and understaffing each lose at least 3 points to the careful roster | over -54.9, under -44.7 | ±3.1, ±3.1 | yes |
| T10 | Reviews matter: tourism_review_blind loses at least 2 points | -7.0 | ±1.8 | yes |
| T12 | 1000 runs complete in under 150 seconds (slowest strategy) | 499.5 s per 1000 runs |  | no |
| T13 | A player without scenario-specific bot knowledge can win: tourism_naive_careful wins at least 20% and no more than 10 points less than tourism_careful | 60.8%; gap +5.7 | ±3.0; ±2.0 | yes |
| T14 | Every staff contract kind wins somewhere: on the seeds where its single-kind bot signs it, permanent, seasonal and part time each beat weekly shifts by their paired margin | permanent -0.2 (signed in 63.3%); seasonal -0.7 (signed in 58.8%); part time +1.1 (signed in 98.0%) | ±1.3; ±1.2; ±1.2 | no |
| T15 | L1. An optional loan for later rooms is a real choice: borrowing and funding later rooms from retained cash are within 5 points of each other | second loan +3.8 against none (second loan taken in 45.5% of runs) | ±1.5 | yes |
| T16 | L2. Borrowing for a temptation no segment requires (tourism_overborrow: every item the bank lists and the most working capital, early) loses clearly against borrowing only what the business needs: at least 3 points | -55.1 | ±3.1 | yes |
| T17 | L3. Debt is dangerous: tourism_reckless (the most the bank lends, a temptation included, no repayment-free start) ends with the loan called or bankrupt owing the bank at least 15 points more often than tourism_careful, and tourism_careful misses a payment the business itself could carry in under 3% of runs | ending owing: reckless 100.0%, careful 42.2% (gap +57.8); careful loan-caused misses 2.3% | ±3.1; ±0.9 | yes |
| T18 | L4. The post-mortem names each loan's interest, what it paid for, and whether it was paid back (tourism_careful's borrowers) | 100.0% borrowed; median interest and fees 9439 EUR |  | yes |
| E1 | E1. The established look comes once the company has made it: the careful bot reaches it in at least 40% of its winning runs, in clearly fewer of its losing runs, and in year 2 (median week after 52) | 98.9% of 551 wins, 4.9% of 449 losses; median week 65 | ±0.9, ±2.0; ±1.0 | yes |
| F1 | Quiet-slot aggregator access beats all slots and no aggregator by at least 2 points each | all +0.7; none -41.5 | ±1.9; ±3.1 | no |

## Pacing in weeks 1 to 20

Interviews and the player's own spending do not count. The stricter column counts only news that asks for a decision: a new message, a lead that moved stage, an experiment result, a lost customer or invoice, or candidates.

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (499.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (368.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (91.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (199.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (191.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (456.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (422.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (440.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (12.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (365.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (373.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (472.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (435.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (428.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (447.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (433.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (446.9 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (177.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (445.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (175.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (459.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (20.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (19.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_aggregator_all`: Opens every slot to aggregator check-ins. (441.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_aggregator_none`: Never joins the aggregator. (189.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_january_peak`: Keeps the extra classes bought for January waitlists. (366.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_reformer_always`: Funds and builds reformers after eight open weeks regardless of interview interest. (353.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_reformer_never`: Keeps the regular mat studio and never buys reformers. (452.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_never_match`: Never matches the most-followed instructor’s offer. (441.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_recovery_early`: Funds and builds the recovery room in the first year. (403.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `fitness_no_later_loan`: Uses the same initial financing and no later loan. (414.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (377.2 s for 1000 runs)

# Griffig, long mode

Generated by `pnpm bots --runs 1000 --seed-prefix test --scenario griffig --mode long`, engine 0.14.0. Every strategy plays the same seeds.

## Strategies

- `tourism_careful`: Interviews every segment in week 1; in week 2 a pilot and, for a segment that books on platforms, a test listing where the interviews point. Picks the segment its evidence favours, builds a product that sells to it and what its interviewees ask for, lists on the platform where that segment books there, sets up its partnerships, builds the website and does outreach where those reach it, prices from interview budget hints (peak at their mean, off season at 85%), rosters to this week's bookings plus a tenth (taking up to two shifts itself), fixes snags, chases invoices, and turns strict only once cancellations are frequent and the calendar busy. It takes a bigger place when guests were turned away in 3 of the last 8 weeks (at the last stage, in peak weeks or above four fifths full, and only with someone on contract where the place needs it) and cash and savings cover the move, six months of the added rent and two months of costs; else it lists the place in the bank's plan. (808.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_platform_only`: Plays like tourism_careful but sells only through the platform: no partnerships, website or outreach. (659.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overstaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for every product's full capacity, booked or not. (1021.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_understaffing`: Plays like tourism_careful but rosters for 60% of what is booked. (605.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_no_tests`: No interviews or tests: builds every product, the temptation included, and sells to the first segment. (34.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_price_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but keeps the starting price all year. (747.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_review_blind`: Plays like tourism_careful but never fixes snags and rosters exactly to bookings, without a margin. (666.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_flexible`: Plays like tourism_careful with a flexible policy all run. (740.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overworker`: Plays like tourism_careful but plans 65 h every week. (11.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but leaves every message to its default. (470.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_inbox_effort`: Plays like tourism_careful but answers messages with blind effort: the choice with the most hours it can afford. (660.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_naive_careful`: Plays like tourism_careful without scenario knowledge beyond the screen: messages answered by their declared conditions only, no bridge months or lead times for the loan, and every channel tried for its groups instead of the channel table (tourism fixes, item 17). (812.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_contracts_none`: Plays like tourism_careful but books every staff shift by the week and signs no contracts. (730.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_permanent`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only permanent staff contracts where steady work supports them. (750.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_seasonal`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only seasonal staff contracts where the current season supports them. (728.2 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_part_time`: Plays like tourism_careful but signs only part time staff contracts where steady work supports them. (730.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but declines every offer of a bigger place (plan 3, target O1). (722.1 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_move`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, and lists it in the bank plan where there is one (plan 3, target O2). (606.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_never_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but never opens a second site or takes a second boat (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (743.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_second`: Plays like tourism_careful but accepts every offer of a bigger place at once, so it opens a second site as early as a run can, working there itself while no manager is on contract, and takes a second boat at the first offer (plan 6, targets S1 and B1). (614.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_always_strict`: Plays like tourism_careful with a strict policy all run. (698.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_overborrow`: Plays like tourism_careful but asks the bank for the most it lends, as soon as the plan is written. (33.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_reckless`: Plays like tourism_careful but borrows the most the bank lends, with no repayment-free start and the shortest term. (29.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_boulder_first`: Builds the bouldering wall first. (1034.3 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_rope_first`: Builds rope walls first. (512.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_both_first`: Builds bouldering and rope walls together. (214.7 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_setting_half`: Allocates half the careful setting hours. (675.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_setting_double`: Allocates twice the careful setting hours. (740.0 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_aggregator_all`: Opens every staffed slot to the aggregator. (744.6 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_aggregator_none`: Never joins the aggregator. (701.8 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_rope_no_courses`: Builds rope walls first without belay or lead courses. (158.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_speed_early`: Funds the speed wall in the first year. (720.5 s for 1000 runs)
- `climbing_no_later_loan`: Keeps initial financing and takes no later loan. (558.4 s for 1000 runs)
- `tourism_refit_loan`: Plays like tourism_careful and takes a second loan for the own fit-out once guests pay about 20,000 EUR a month. (521.6 s for 1000 runs)

# Tuning notes

## Leinen los: targets that do not apply (tuning 1)

- T9 (staffing): the only role is the host, one a week; the crew is a cost per week sailed. Nothing to over- or understaff.
- T14 (contracts): no staff contracts, for the same reason.
- T15 to T18 (the loan targets): the bank lends only to owners, and the careful bot never buys, so it never borrows.
- T19 to T21 (office stages): one stage only.
- T4 is restated for runs that start out of season (the first season's first month); see `docs/decisions.md`, tuning 1 step 1.

Tuning steps per scenario, newest last. Each one lists the change, the reason, and the effect on the targets. Details are in `docs/decisions.md`.

## Kantino

### Baseline (engine 0.3.0, before any tuning)

Sustainable endings: validate_then_build 92.9%, overworker 96.4%, sell_first 61.8%, playtest 41.0% (98.1% when coworking is viable), build_only 0%. The median losing run lasted 56 weeks. Targets 2, 3 and 4 failed. The full baseline report is in the commit that added the bots.

### 1. Interview signals less clean

Trap politeness 0.28 to 0.35 became 0.50 to 0.65, mediocre 0.15 to 0.30 became 0.25 to 0.40, viable 0.10 to 0.20 became 0.05 to 0.15. Interview noise 0.20 became 0.30, interviewing skill gain 0.02 became 0.01, the Urgent bucket starts at 0.90 instead of 0.80, and the feature mention chance became 0.3 + 0.3 x pain (was 0.5 x pain). Reason: in the playtest the viable market answered Urgent 5 of 5 times in week 1. Effect: after 6 interviews per market the viable one ranks first in 62% of runs (was 93%), with 44% Urgent answers (was 65%). Win rates barely moved (validate_then_build 89.7%), because bots that pick wrong still recover by moving on.

### 2. Lead volume halved

Every channel response range was halved. Reason: 45 h of in person outreach found 7 to 8 leads a week in the playtest, and section 10 of the scenario expects about one lead per 15 h early on. Effect: leads per outreach hour fell from 0.22 to 0.105 for validate_then_build (0.067 at the start, rising with selling skill). validate_then_build 78.0%, overworker 84.3%, sell_first 43.2%, playtest 16.7% (50.9% when coworking is viable, median 2 customers at week 15 there instead of 4).

### 3. Less starting cash

Company cash 15,000 became 10,000 EUR. With 12,000 EUR savings the founder has about 42 weeks of runway without revenue (was 52). Reason: losing runs lasted a median of 55 weeks, above target 4 (20 to 50), and the runway left room for many wrong turns. Effect: target 4 met (week 44). validate_then_build 73.1%, overworker 80.5%, sell_first 41.0%, playtest 15.1%.

### 4. Willingness to pay evened out across markets

Small offices 150 to 400 became 200 to 450 EUR, medical practices 100 to 250 became 180 to 320 EUR. Reason: at the default price of 300 EUR the medical market converted at about a quarter of the rate (price factor around 0.25), so validate_then_build won only 11% of runs where medical was viable against 75% where coworking was. Which market was viable decided the run more than the player did. Effect: wins by viable market for validate_then_build are now 89/95/82% (small offices/coworking/medical), and overall win rates rose (validate_then_build 88.6%). The next step brings them down for all markets alike.

### 5. Leakier funnel, viable markets slightly less in pain

Lead to demo 0.25 became 0.18 x (0.4 + 0.6 x pain), demo to trial 0.20 + 0.60 x fit became 0.05 + 0.40 x fit, selling skill gain per demo 0.01 became 0.005, viable pain 0.65 to 0.90 became 0.60 to 0.85, and churn 0.02 + 0.25 x misfit became 0.03 + 0.20 x misfit. Reason: a bot that found the viable market ended with about 19 customers, while 7 make the company profitable, so finding the market was the whole game. A version with churn 0.06 + 0.45 x misfit also met the targets but meant 20 to 30% monthly churn for a product that fits, which feels like a leaky bucket. It was rejected in favour of a slower funnel. Effect: all five targets met. validate_then_build 37.7%, overworker 55.4%, sell_first 17.5%, playtest 0.1% (it works about 20 h a week and never fixes bugs). The viable market now ranks first after 6 interviews per market in 52% of runs.

### 6. Low energy penalty and burnout (formula, from scenario section 7)

The two energy rules the scenario already specified now work: below 30 energy every sales chance is multiplied by 0.85, and three weeks in a row at 0 end the run with burnout. They were planned for Phase 4. Reason: energy had no effect, so planning 65 h every week was free. The overworker won 55.4% against 37.7% for the same play at 50 h, and the playtest showed energy climbing to 100 and staying there unused. Scenario values could not fix this, because no value made energy matter. Effect: the overworker burns out in week 7 of every run, as the plan expects. Other strategies are unchanged, because they stay at or under 50 h. Target 3 now has a 22 point margin (best strategy: validate_then_build 37.7%).

### Phase 3 review: new measurements, no tuning

Added after the review: ending week histogram, earnings of losing runs, week of the first paying customer, the `price_aware` bot, and targets 6 and 7 (not tuned toward yet). Findings at engine 0.4.0, seeds `test` (the `holdout` seeds agree within 2 points):

- Every bankruptcy falls in weeks 41 to 60, most in 41 to 50. No run dies early, so a losing run's length is set by the starting money, not by the player's choices.
- Few losing validate_then_build runs earned nothing: 9.1% never received a payment and 6.6% never had a customer. Most died with 1 to 6 customers. 11.1% reached 2,070 EUR monthly revenue at some point and still lost, from churn or by not holding it for two full months before money ran out.
- Losing playtest runs never got paid in 40.7% of cases.
- The first paying customer arrives in week 13 (median) in winning validate_then_build runs, and week 18 in losing ones. Target 7 holds, near its upper edge.
- Win rates by viable market for validate_then_build: coworking 54.4%, small offices 36.3%, medical 23.6%. The spread of 30.8 points fails target 6.
- `price_aware` does not beat the plain version: -4.4 to +0.6 points per market. In coworking its winning runs end at a median of 700 EUR, so higher prices hold there, but the search is slow (4 trial results per step) and costs conversions in the cheaper markets. The pricing advantage flagged in tuning 4 is small for this rule; a faster or bolder rule is untested.

### Phase 4: new systems, no tuning

Engine 0.5.0, seeds `test`. Invoices, bookkeeping, landing tests, pre-sales, hire and inbox arrived with no value changed to compensate. Bots do the books, chase three invoices a week, and `validate_then_build` runs a small landing test per market in week 1 and up to two pre-sale batches.

- Target 2 fails: validate_then_build wins 17.5% (was 37.7%). Customers now pay 2 to 4 weeks after the month end, the books cost 2 h a week, and week 1 spends 600 EUR on ads.
- Bankruptcies come earlier for `random` (weeks 31 to 40 for 91%). Median losing run is week 42 (was 43), still inside target 4.
- Target 6 spread is 25.1 points (was 30.8). Coworking still wins most often. Not tuned toward.
- Target 7 holds at week 13.
- Bots ignore the inbox (default choices happen), except `random`. Event balance is therefore unmeasured.

### Careful bot report (engine 0.6.0, no tuning)

`validate_then_build` was redefined to play the Phase 4 systems competently (see `docs/decisions.md`, "Careful bot"). Its win rate returned to 26.7% (17.5% for the previous bot) and the hire variant is at 12.8%. No scenario value or formula changed; the follow up action and the 3 lead pre-sale are engine 0.6.0 changes made before this run. Targets 6 and 7 are still not tuned toward.

### Follow ups and an early hire (engine 0.6.0, no tuning)

Bots only, no scenario value or formula changed. Seeds `test`. `validate_then_build` now follows up with up to six open leads in its market every week (pre-sales were already in). A third variant, `validate_then_build_early_hire`, searches as soon as its own funnel says a hire pays back before week 78.

- Follow ups are the strongest single behaviour: validate_then_build wins 37.7% (26.7% before; 26.7% again with them switched off, -11.0 points).
- Chase (+1.1 without it), landing (-0.6) and books (+0.1) still change nothing measurable. The inbox policy is worth 9.8 points.
- The hire loses in every form. The covered rule hires in week 58 (median) and wins 35.6% of those runs, where plain play wins 98.5% of the same seeds (203 wins lost, none gained). Its salary of 3,800 EUR a month counts in the win condition and is not earned back by week 78. The payback rule hires in only 4.4% of runs, always a salesperson, in week 16 (median), and wins 2.3% of them against 81.8% for plain play on the same seeds. Its estimate is optimistic on small samples, and the salary drains the money by week 30.
- Pacing: in weeks 1 to 20, 94% of bot weeks have something noteworthy and 90% have decision news (a message, a lead that moved, a result). A new message arrives in 74% of weeks. Bots run outreach from about week 4; hand play that reaches outreach later sees less.
- Wins by viable market: coworking 53.5%, small offices 39.2%, medical 21.6% (spread 31.9).

### Engine 0.7.0: design fixes, then three tuning rounds

Design changes as agreed in review (books as a message, invoices that wait for a chase, landing tests with leads and a price signal, a junior hire, gradual energy, reworked inbox outcomes, deposits from cold pre-sales, 1 week trials, a weekly message, 6,000 + 9,500 EUR over 60 weeks, higher willingness to pay in medical and small offices), plus outreach saturation (36 h per channel and market over 3 weeks, then 0.3 per hour). Seeds `test`.

- Before tuning: validate_then_build 27.3%, spread 27.0 points (medical 11.7%), blind inbox effort +6.6 points over no answers, first customer week 5. Payback hires won 0 of 552 runs.
- Round 1: medical cold email 0.04 to 0.08 (was 0.01 to 0.03) and in person 0.06 to 0.12 (was 0.05 to 0.10); coworking in person 0.06 to 0.12 (was 0.075 to 0.15). Hearing out the friend of a friend or the restaurant owner costs extra hours later, studying the competitor costs energy. Spread 8.6 points; T10 measured at 8 of 23 events; blind effort +7.1.
- Round 2: doing nothing about a crash report risks churn 0.1 (was 0.25), an apology 0.05 (was 0.1). Blind effort +6.7, almost no change.
- Round 3: a referral brings 1 lead (was 2); promising the cafeteria integration also loses 3 h. Blind effort +3.7, reading +13.3. All targets hold: validate_then_build 26.7%, spread 1.2 points, first customer week 5, evidence by week 10 in 93.4% of runs, 10 of 23 events favour the cheap answer, in person outreach finds 34.3% of customers.
- Target 12 (hires pay back) was dropped after review. Hire variants are shown for information: they win far less than plain play, as intended for a choice that rarely pays.

Every monetary constant was multiplied by 1.5 (see `docs/decisions.md`), and the developer works 20 h instead of 25. Seeds `test`, before and after:

| # | Target | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|

- Phase 5 (engine 0.8.0, `--events`): 108 events, all once except the books. With the new events as first written, validate_then_build fell to 24.6%, blind effort rose to +4.8 and switching off the books cost 0.5 points. Changes: situational lead events now cost 2 to 3 h on follow ups when more than 8 leads are open, the books weight went from 3 to 40 (about 7 arrivals a run again), and the reader migrates the hosting, switches the bank and skips the uptime monitor (monthly costs cost several points each). Result: validate_then_build 25.8%, effort +3.9, cheapest +1.7, reading +11.8, books -3.7, no repeats, a dry inbox week in 0.1% of runs. Seed prefix `check` gives 25.9%, effort +1.7, cheapest -2.6, reading +9.8.

### Phase 7: coworking desk (engine 0.9.0)

The desk offer arrives for sure in week 8 or after the first customer; `validate_then_build` takes it once it has a paying customer and cash is not tight.

| Desk | validate_then_build | Note |
|---|---|---|

With the offer first counted as an open message, blind effort drifted to +4.7 (target 11) even though no blind policy takes the desk; triggered messages now sit outside the draw and it is back to +3.9. Answering it the same way every time: move 26.1%, stay 25.8%.

The organised stage first arrived with the first customer (company costs are 405 EUR a month, one customer pays 450), weeks 4 to 6. Its trigger is now revenue covering all costs, you included, or the first hire. Week it is reached, 1000 seeds:

### Coworking desk: does it matter anywhere? (analysis, no tuning)

Each seed played twice, forced to take the desk and forced to decline it, everything else the same; 1000 seeds each of validate_then_build, presale_max, price_aware, sell_first, build_only, playtest and random. Splits by the declining run: outreach per week in the 10 weeks after the offer (thirds), cash at the offer (lowest third below 8,295 EUR), whether a product had shipped. "z" is the sign test on seeds won by only one of the two choices; within about 2 is chance.

| Split | Runs | Win: take | Win: decline | z | Weeks survived, take minus decline |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| All | 7000 | 10.7% | 10.9% | -0.5 | -1.55 |
| Outreach low (under 15 h a week) | 1996 | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0 | -1.31 |
| Outreach medium | 2658 | 4.9% | 5.1% | -0.6 | -1.59 |
| Outreach high (37 h a week or more) | 2346 | 26.5% | 26.7% | -0.3 | -1.71 |
| Cash tight | 2309 | 2.3% | 2.0% | 0.7 | -1.48 |
| Cash comfortable | 4691 | 14.9% | 15.3% | -0.7 | -1.58 |
| Product shipped | 6535 | 11.5% | 11.7% | -0.5 | -1.57 |
| Nothing shipped | 465 | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.0 | -1.23 |
| validate_then_build | 1000 | 26.1% | 25.8% | 0.2 | -2.08 |
| presale_max | 1000 | 22.5% | 25.3% | -2.0 | -1.51 |

The desk never changes who wins, and every losing run ends one to two weeks sooner with it. For validate_then_build alone the splits are empty: it always has a product and more than 6,000 EUR at the offer and does about 38 h of outreach.

### Phase 9 part B: the freelancer (engine 0.12.0, no tuning)

The freelancer adds no random draws while unused: every table above is unchanged under engine 0.12.0 (10,000 seeds for main and paired, compared number for number). Target 19 and the section "The freelancer (target 19)" are new; the leave-one-out table now names its rows. A free freelance developer for the build is worth about 1 point, a free salesperson for 8 weeks about 2; at 1.4 times the hire's hourly cost (68 and 58 EUR) both lose 6 to 8 points. Target 19 was restated after an energy check (owner's option A): renting never beats doing the work yourself (-5.9 Kantino, -6.6 Zollstock) and costs less than an early hire (-21.5, -24.8). It passes in both scenarios; see `docs/decisions.md`.

## Zollstock

### Part B: first content, no tuning (engine 0.10.0)

Built from the spec with Kantino's tuned values, Kantino's final desk (375 EUR, 0.9 small crews walk-ins a week), referrals at 0.08 x fit minus 0.01 per bug and x 1.6 on demo chances, and 43 own events that each take the mechanics of a Kantino twin. `validate_then_build` wins 12.7% (target 25 to 45%); the viable markets spread 15.4 points (small crews 20.6%, contractors 12.3%, maintenance 5.2%); blind effort is +4.6 over no answers; switching off landing tests gains 5.8 points (the bot's 7% sign up bar was set for Kantino's landing rates, twice Zollstock's); presale_max beats it by 8.5. Referrals bring 14.3% of customers in winning runs, walk-ins 19.3%. No inbox pool runs dry except one validate_then_build run in week 59 (0.1%).

Desk, taken against declined on the same 1000 seeds (`pnpm bots --desk --scenario zollstock`), by the real role of small crews: viable +7.7 points (z 3.5), mediocre -3.3 (z -2.3), trap -5.2 (z -3.1). With Kantino's numbers this reproduces the Phase 7 table exactly.

### Part C: balancing (engine 0.10.0, scenario values only)

Quick rounds are 1000 seeds without `--events`; the final row is the full report above.

| Step | validate_then_build | Spread (crews / contractors / maintenance) | Effort | Landing | Books | presale_max |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Part B | 12.7% | 15.4 (20.6 / 12.3 / 5.2) | +4.6 | +5.8 | -2.2 | +8.5 |
| Step 1: landing bar 3.7% (bot) | 19.1% | 19.9 (29.8 / 17.6 / 9.9) | +5.7 | -0.6 | -2.3 | +2.1 |
| Round 1: maintenance channels; leads in `site_visit`, `returning_prospect` need room | 20.2% | 10.9 (25.4 / 14.5 / 20.4) | +5.2 | -0.3 | -1.5 | +2.6 |
| Round 2: referrals 0.11 x fit; contractors in person | 23.7% | 11.9 (29.8 / 17.9 / 23.0) | +3.5 | -2.4 | -3.4 | +3.5 |
| Careful pre-sales: every batch in the chosen market (bot) | 29.8% | 16.6 (39.2 / 22.6 / 27.1) | +8.3 | +2.8 | -1.2 | -2.6 |
| Round 3: small crews in person down, contractors phone up; offline mode 10 h; books fine 750 EUR; thesis costs energy | 30.4% | 12.7 (38.6 / 26.4 / 25.9) | +4.8 | -1.0 | -4.6 | -5.5 |

Still failing after three rounds:

- Target 11, blind effort +4.8 (limit 4). Leads and fewer lost customers are worth more in Zollstock than in Kantino, because outreach is harder (cold email barely works, so two channels share the saturation), and many events reward effort with exactly those. The Kantino fix (situational effects, costly wasteful answers) brought it from +8.3 to +4.8 in one round; one more round on the remaining effort winners (`crash_report`, `apprentice_setup`, `guild_evening`, the shared `regional_grant` and `security_notice`) is likely to close it.
- Target 13, landing tests: switching them off costs 1.0 point (target 2). Zollstock's landing test is half as informative by design (spec section 5), and the careful bot drops a market on one noisy test below a fixed bar. The relative rule planned for Phase 8 should fix the bot side; if landing tests stay weak, the target may need to say "at least 2 points where the scenario makes them informative".

Also worth knowing: in winning runs referrals bring 19.1% of customers (largest source in person, 27.1%); the first paying customer comes in week 5; the inbox runs dry in 0.1% of validate_then_build and presale_max runs (week 59). The desk after tuning, taken against declined: small crews viable +20.1 (z 7.0), mediocre -7.7 (z -2.9), trap -10.5 (z -3.8). It clearly helps and hurts as intended, perhaps more strongly than Kantino's (+10.7 / -13.6 / -9.6); the viable markets' spread holds at 12.7 because the reader only takes it when its evidence puts small crews first.

### After part C: events round, reverted (no change to this report)

Four events (crash_report, apprentice_setup, guild_evening, the shared regional_grant) were made situational to bring blind effort under 4. It rose instead: Zollstock +4.8 to +5.5 on the `test` seeds (+5.9 to +7.0 on 3000 seeds, reading 31.2% to 26.4%), and Kantino's target 11 failed at +4.6 through the shared grant. Reverted; both reports above stand. Details in `docs/decisions.md`.
